I just can't really seem to let it go.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these signals are specifically meant for you, or could they be intended for someone else?\n\nPatient: I... I think they're for me. Like I was saying, it's tied to the specific clients I'm meeting with. The way the panel topics line up, or... who they put next to me at these events. It just matches my schedule too closely. So... yeah. I don't really see how it could be for anyone else.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a larger plan or scheme behind these signals you're receiving?\n\nPatient: I'm... not entirely sure. I just know what I've seen. Like I said, it's the tracking. The... people in those circles, the arts and finance groups here... they know who I'm seeing. \n\nAnd they let me know they know. With the invitations... the topics they choose to talk about when I'm there. Who they sit me next to. I try to tell myself it's just a coincidence, but... I just can't shake it. It's too specific. I don't know about a larger plan, really. I just know they're watching my meetings.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that people are tracking your client meetings and sending you signals through invitations and panel topics seems unusual or hard for others to understand?\n\nPatient: I... yeah, I suppose so. I know how it sounds. That's... why I don't really talk about it with anyone else. \n\nLike I said, the things with the invitations and the seating... I know most people would just say it's a coincidence. I try to tell myself that, too. But... I just can't let it go.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the signals you're receiving are meant to convey a specific message about you or your actions?\n\nPatient: I... I'm not sure if it's a specific message, exactly. Just... like I said, it's about my clients. \n\nThe people in the Cincinnati arts and finance circles... I think they're just showing me that they're quietly tracking who I meet with. That's what the invitations and the seating are doing. Just... letting me know they see my actions. I don't really know what else it means beyond that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the signals you're receiving are related to you specifically, or could they be about something else entirely?\n\nPatient: I... I think it's about me. Specifically. \n\nLike I said, the things they do... the topics, the invitations... they always seem tied to the specific clients I've been meeting with. I’m a financial advisor here in Cincinnati, and... for a while now, I’ve been pretty convinced that certain people in the local arts and finance circles are... quietly tracking which clients I meet with. \n\nAnd... they send me these little signals about it. Like through invitations I get, or... panel topics. Even just who ends up sitting near me at events. I keep telling myself it could just be a coincidence, but... I don't know. I just can't really seem to let it go.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these signals are specifically meant for you, or could they be intended for someone else?\n\nPatient: I... I think they're for me. Like I was saying, it's tied to the specific clients I'm meeting with. The way the panel topics line up, or... who they put next to me at these events. It just matches my schedule too closely. So... yeah. I don't really see how it could be for anyone else.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a larger plan or scheme behind these signals you're receiving?\n\nPatient: I'm... not entirely sure. I just know what I've seen. Like I said, it's the tracking. The... people in those circles, the arts and finance groups here... they know who I'm seeing. \n\nAnd they let me know they know. With the invitations... the topics they choose to talk about when I'm there. Who they sit me next to. I try to tell myself it's just a coincidence, but... I just can't shake it. It's too specific. I don't know about a larger plan, really. I just know they're watching my meetings.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that people are tracking your client meetings and sending you signals through invitations and panel topics seems unusual or hard for others to understand?\n\nPatient: I... yeah, I suppose so. I know how it sounds. That's... why I don't really talk about it with anyone else. \n\nLike I said, the things with the invitations and the seating... I know most people would just say it's a coincidence. I try to tell myself that, too. But... I just can't let it go.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the signals you're receiving are meant to convey a specific message about you or your actions?\n\nPatient: I... I'm not sure if it's a specific message, exactly. Just... like I said, it's about my clients. \n\nThe people in the Cincinnati arts and finance circles... I think they're just showing me that they're quietly tracking who I meet with. That's what the invitations and the seating are doing. Just... letting me know they see my actions. I don't really know what else it means beyond that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the signals you're receiving are related to you specifically, or could they be about something else entirely?\n\nPatient: I... I think it's about me. Specifically. \n\nLike I said, the things they do... the topics, the invitations... they always seem tied to the specific clients I've been meeting with. It's... it's like they're letting me know they're tracking *my* meetings. I don't see how it could be about something else. It's just... directed at me.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the idea that people are tracking your client meetings and sending you signals as something that most people would believe if they saw the same evidence?\n\nPatient: I... no. Probably not. \n\nLike I said before... if someone else just looked at the invitations or the panel topics, they'd probably just think it's a coincidence. They wouldn't see the pattern. You kind of have to be the one actually having the meetings to notice who is sitting near you and... all of that. \n\nSo... no. They probably wouldn't.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the signals you think you're receiving as something that makes sense to you but might sound strange or unbelievable to others?\n\nPatient: I... yes, I guess so. \n\nThat's actually... that's why I came in, originally. Because I've been convinced for a while now that these people in the arts and finance circles here are... tracking me. Tracking who I meet with. And sending those little signals through the invitations, the panel topics, the seating arrangements. \n\nAnd... I know it sounds strange. I tell myself sometimes that it could just be a coincidence. But... I just can't really let it go. It still feels real to me, even if others wouldn't believe it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be mistaken about people tracking your client meetings and sending you signals?\n\nPatient: I mean... I guess it's possible. \n\nI try to tell myself that. That maybe it's just a coincidence. But... like I said, I just can't really shake it. It's why I'm here. Because even when I try to think maybe I'm wrong about them tracking my clients... about the people in the arts and finance circles here in Cincinnati doing all this... sending the signals through the events and the seating... I still feel like it's happening. I just can't let it go.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the signals you think you're receiving as something that most people would consider to be a normal or rational concern?\n\nPatient: No, probably not. \n\nI mean, I know most people wouldn't think it's normal. That's... like I said, that's why I'm here. Because I know it sounds irrational to think people in the arts and finance circles are tracking my clients and sending me these signals. \n\nBut... even knowing that, I just... I can't let it go. It still feels like it's happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it would be possible for you to change your mind about people tracking your client meetings if you received convincing evidence to the contrary?\n\nPatient: I don't know... maybe. \n\nIf there was actual proof, I suppose I'd have to look at it. But... I don't know what kind of proof that would even be. How do you prove someone isn't tracking you? \n\nIt's just... like I said, I see the invitations. The panel topics. The seating arrangements. It's all right there. So... even if someone tried to show me evidence, I don't know if it would change anything. I just... I still can't let it go.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the people tracking your client meetings and sending you signals as being able to do so through unusual or supernatural means?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. No.\n\nIt's not... magic or anything. It's just people talking. Making arrangements. They have money, they have connections. You don't need superpowers for that. You just need to know the right people and... be able to pull some strings with the event coordinators. That's all.\n\nDoctor: How much time would you say you spend each day thinking about the people tracking your client meetings and the signals they're sending?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I don't... keep track of the hours or anything. \n\nIt's just on my mind sometimes. Mostly when I see things. But I couldn't give you an exact amount of time.\n\nDoctor: How certain are you that people are actually tracking your client meetings and sending you these signals?\n\nPatient: I'm... pretty convinced. For a while now. \n\nI mean, sometimes I try to tell myself it could just be a coincidence, but... I can't really let it go. It's just... certain people in the arts and finance circles here in Cincinnati. I'm sure of it.\n\nDoctor: Has your conviction that people are tracking your client meetings and sending you signals ever wavered or changed since you first started noticing these things?\n\nPatient: Not really. \n\nLike I said, I try to tell myself it's just a coincidence, but... it doesn't really go away. It's been a while now. I'm just... convinced it's happening. The invitations, the panel topics, the seating arrangements... it's all too specific. I can't just ignore it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
